Repeated\n * failures are counted per username by {@link LoginAttemptTracker}; crossing the\n * threshold suspends authentication for that account for a while, correct password or\n * not.\n *\n * <p>Security properties this class is responsible for:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>Passwords are stored only as PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 digests with a per-account\n *       random salt, never in plaintext, and are compared in constant time.</li>\n *   <li>Every refusal is worded the same way except for the lock notice, and every\n *       refusal costs the same amount of work, so a caller cannot learn whether a\n *       username exists from either the message or the response time.</li>\n *   <li>Username and password are shape-validated before anything touches the account\n *       store, so a backing store never receives a value that cannot be an account\n *       key.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>Everything is in the default package and in this one file so that the whole\n * implementation can be read, compiled and run as a unit.\n */\npublic final class LoginService {\n\n    /** Refusal wording shared by every non-lock rejection, so the reason is not leaked. */\n    public static final String INVALID_MESSAGE = \"Invalid username or password.\";\n\n    /**\n     * Username shape: 1-64 characters, alphanumeric first, then alphanumerics or\n     * {@code . _ -}. Matching is exact - no trimming and no case folding - because\n     * normalising would merge distinct inputs into one account key. Anything else is\n     * rejected before the store is consulted.\n     */\n    private static final Pattern USERNAME = Pattern.compile(\"[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,63}\");\n\n    public enum LoginStatus { SUCCESS, INVALID_CREDENTIALS, ACCOUNT_LOCKED }\n\n    /** Result of one {@link LoginService#login} call. */\n    public static final class LoginOutcome {\n\n        private final LoginStatus status;\n        private final String message;\n        private final long retryInSeconds;\n\n        private LoginOutcome(LoginStatus status, String message, long retryInSeconds) {\n            this.status = status;\n            this.message = message;\n            this.retryInSeconds = retryInSeconds;\n        }\n\n        public LoginStatus status() { return status; }\n\n        /** Wording safe to show the person attempting to log in. */\n        public String message() { return message; }\n\n        /** True only for {@link LoginStatus#SUCCESS}; grant access on this and nothing else. */\n        public boolean granted() { return status == LoginStatus.SUCCESS; }\n\n        /** Seconds until authentication is retried for a locked account; 0 otherwise. */\n        public long retryInSeconds() { return retryInSeconds; }\n\n        @Override public String toString() {\n            return status + (retryInSeconds > 0 ? \" (retry in \" + retryInSeconds + \"s)\" : \"\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Read side of the account directory. Implementations that talk to a database or an\n     * LDAP server must keep the credential comparison above the constant-time path in\n     * {@link PasswordHasher} and must surface lookup failures as {@link Optional#empty()}\n     * only when the account genuinely does not exist - never on error.\n     */\n    public interface AccountStore {\n        Optional<Account> find(String username);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * A stored account. {@code hashedPassword} is a {@link PasswordHasher} encoded\n     * record, never the password itself.\n     */\n    public record Account(String username, String hashedPassword, boolean enabled) {}\n\n    /** Hook for recording authentication activity. It must never be given the password. */\n    public interface AuditSink {\n\n        AuditSink NOOP = (username, status, retryInSeconds) -> { };\n\n        void record(String username, LoginStatus status, long retryInSeconds);\n\n        /** Sink that prints one line per attempt; usernames only, never credentials. */\n        static AuditSink println() {\n            return (username, status, retryInSeconds) -> System.out.println(\"    audit \"\n                    + status + \" username=\" + username + \" retryInSeconds=\" + retryInSeconds);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private final AccountStore accounts;\n    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    private final LoginAttemptTracker tracker;\n    private final AuditSink audit;\n\n    public LoginService(AccountStore accounts, PasswordHasher hasher, LoginAttemptTracker tracker) {\n        this(accounts, hasher, tracker, AuditSink.NOOP);\n    }\n\n    public LoginService(AccountStore accounts, PasswordHasher hasher, LoginAttemptTracker tracker,\n                        AuditSink audit) {\n        if (accounts == null || hasher == null || tracker == null || audit == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"collaborators must not be null\");\n        }\n        this.accounts = accounts;\n        this.hasher = hasher;\n        this.tracker = tracker;\n        this.audit = audit;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Verifies a submitted credential pair.\n     *\n     * <p>Every path through this method that does not grant access performs exactly one\n     * full key derivation - either against the account's real record or against a\n     * throwaway record built at {@link PasswordHasher} construction - so that a refusal\n     * for an unknown username, a malformed name, an unusable password or a locked\n     * account takes the same time as a genuine password check. Removing any one of\n     * those charges turns login into an account-enumeration oracle, which is why the\n     * test suite times each of the five refusal shapes separately.\n     *\n     * @param username   candidate username, matched exactly\n     * @param password   candidate password; the array is left untouched, so the caller\n     *                   stays free to overwrite it once the call returns\n     * @return the outcome; only {@link LoginStatus#SUCCESS} authorises access\n     */\n    public LoginOutcome login(String username, char[] password) {\n        if (!isWellFormedUsername(username)) {\n            hasher.burnWork();\n            return refuse(null, LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, 0L);\n        }\n\n        long lockedFor = tracker.remainingLockMillis(username);\n        if (lockedFor > 0L) {\n            // Refusing early would make the lock state readable by stopwatch.\n            hasher.burnWork();\n            return refuse(username, LoginStatus.ACCOUNT_LOCKED, secondsUp(lockedFor));\n        }\n\n        if (!PasswordHasher.isHashable(password)) {\n            hasher.burnWork();\n            tracker.recordFailure(username);\n            return refuse(username, LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, 0L);\n        }\n\n        Optional<Account> found = accounts.find(username);\n        if (found.isEmpty() || !found.get().enabled()) {\n            // Derive against a record that cannot match anything: same cost, same answer\n            // as a wrong password, so neither time nor wording says \"no such user\".\n            hasher.burnWork();\n            tracker.recordFailure(username);\n            return refuse(username, LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, 0L);\n        }\n\n        if (hasher.matches(password, found.get().hashedPassword())) {\n            tracker.recordSuccess(username);\n            audit.record(username, LoginStatus.SUCCESS, 0L);\n            return new LoginOutcome(LoginStatus.SUCCESS, \"Access granted.\", 0L);\n        }\n\n        // The failure that crosses the threshold still reports INVALID_CREDENTIALS; the\n        // lock becomes visible on the next attempt. Counting the trip here and reporting\n        // it there keeps the two responses worded identically for as long as the\n        // submitted password is simply wrong.\n        tracker.recordFailure(username);\n        return refuse(username, LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, 0L);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Whether {@code username} can name an account at all. A malformed name is not a\n     * login attempt against anybody, so it is refused without consulting the store or\n     * accumulating failures under an attacker-chosen key.\n     */\n    public static boolean isWellFormedUsername(String username) {\n        return username != null && USERNAME.matcher(username).matches();\n    }\n\n    private LoginOutcome refuse(String username, LoginStatus status, long retryInSeconds) {\n        audit.record(username, status, retryInSeconds);\n        return new LoginOutcome(status, INVALID_MESSAGE.equals(INVALID_MESSAGE)\n                ? (status == LoginStatus.ACCOUNT_LOCKED ? \"Account temporarily locked. Try again later.\"\n                                                        : INVALID_MESSAGE)\n                : INVALID_MESSAGE, retryInSeconds);\n    }\n\n    private static long secondsUp(long millis) {\n        return (millis + 999L) / 1000L;\n    }\n\n    // ------------------------------------------------------------------ hashing\n\n    /**\n     * PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 password records in the form\n     * {@code pbkdf2$<iterations>$<base64 salt>$<base64 key>}.\n     *\n     * <p>{@link #matches(char[], String)} re-derives with the iteration count embedded in\n     * the record, so stored records keep verifying after the cost parameter is raised for\n     * new passwords.\n     */\n    public static final class PasswordHasher {\n\n        private static final String PREFIX = \"pbkdf2\";\n        private static final String ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n        private static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n        private static final int KEY_BITS = 256;\n\n        /** Stored iteration counts outside this range are treated as a corrupt record. */\n        public static final int MIN_ITERATIONS = 1_000;\n        public static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 5_000_000;\n\n        /** Cost of a new password; about 0.3s per derivation on a 2026-class x86 core. */\n        public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 210_000;\n\n        private final int iterations;\n        private final SecureRandom random = new SecureRandom();\n\n        /**\n         * A real record derived from a random password that no caller can present.\n         * {@link #burnWork()} verifies against it, which is what makes the equalised\n         * refusal paths cost the same as a real check. A hand-written constant would not\n         * do: if it failed to parse, the equalising derivation would never run and the\n         * branch would silently cost nothing.\n         */\n        private final String dummyRecord;\n        private final char[] dummyPassword;\n\n        public PasswordHasher() {\n            this(DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n        }\n\n        public PasswordHasher(int iterations) {\n            if (iterations < MIN_ITERATIONS || iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"iterations out of range: \" + iterations);\n            }\n            this.iterations = iterations;\n            byte[] seed = new byte[24];\n            this.random.nextBytes(seed);\n            this.dummyPassword = Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding().encodeToString(seed).toCharArray();\n            this.dummyRecord = hash(this.dummyPassword);\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Whether {@code password} can be hashed without the JCE silently changing it.\n         *\n         * <p>{@link PBEKeySpec} converts the {@code char[]} with its own UTF-8 encoder,\n         * which is not injective over the whole domain: trailing NULs are dropped and\n         * every unpaired surrogate becomes {@code ?}. Those inputs therefore collide with\n         * other, shorter passwords, so one person's secret would verify as another's. They\n         * are refused rather than trimmed, because trimming would quietly change the\n         * secret. Interior NULs and correctly paired surrogates are fine.\n         */\n        public static boolean isHashable(char[] password) {\n            if (password == null || password.length == 0 || password.length > 1_024) {\n                return false;\n            }\n            if (password[password.length - 1] == '\\0') {\n                return false;\n            }\n            for (int i = 0; i < password.length; i++) {\n                char c = password[i];\n                if (Character.isHighSurrogate(c)) {\n                    if (i + 1 >= password.length || !Character.isLowSurrogate(password[i + 1])) {\n                        return false;\n                    }\n                    i++;\n                } else if (Character.isLowSurrogate(c)) {\n                    return false;\n                }\n            }\n            return true;\n        }\n\n        /** Encodes a new password with a fresh random salt. */\n        public String hash(char[] password) {\n            if (!isHashable(password)) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password cannot be hashed safely\");\n            }\n            byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n            random.nextBytes(salt);\n            byte[] key = derive(password, salt, iterations);\n            return PREFIX + \"$\" + iterations + \"$\"\n                    + Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(salt) + \"$\"\n                    + Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(key);\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Re-derives {@code password} under the record's salt and cost and compares in\n         * constant time. A malformed or tampered record yields {@code false} after paying\n         * the normal cost, never an exception, so a corrupt row cannot be probed by\n         * error or by stopwatch.\n         */\n        public boolean matches(char[] password, String stored) {\n            Record record = parse(stored);\n            if (record == null || !isHashable(password)) {\n                burnWork();\n                return false;\n            }\n            byte[] candidate = derive(password, record.salt, record.iterations);\n            return MessageDigest.isEqual(record.key, candidate);\n        }\n\n        /** Spends one derivation on a record that matches nothing, to equalise timing. */\n        public void burnWork() {\n            matches(dummyPassword, dummyRecord);\n        }\n\n        /** Iteration count used for newly hashed passwords. */\n        public int iterations() { return iterations; }\n\n        private static byte[] derive(char[] password, byte[] salt, int iterations) {\n            try {\n                SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(ALGORITHM);\n                PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(password, salt, iterations, KEY_BITS);\n                byte[] key = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n                spec.clearPassword();\n                return key;\n            } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n                throw new IllegalStateException(ALGORITHM + \" unavailable\", e);\n            }\n        }\n\n        private static Record parse(String stored) {\n            if (stored == null) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            String[] parts = stored.split(\"\\\\$\", -1);\n            if (parts.length != 4 || !PREFIX.equals(parts[0])) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            int iterations;\n            try {\n                iterations = Integer.parseInt(parts[1]);\n            } catch (NumberFormatException e) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            if (iterations < MIN_ITERATIONS || iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            byte[] salt;\n            byte[] key;\n            try {\n                salt = Base64.getDecoder().decode(parts[2]);\n                key = Base64.getDecoder().decode(parts[3]);\n            } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            if (salt.length == 0 || salt.length > 64 || key.length != KEY_BITS / 8) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            return new Record(iterations, salt, key);\n        }\n\n        private record Record(int iterations, byte[] salt, byte[] key) {}\n    }\n\n    // ----------------------------------------------------------------- lockout\n\n    /**\n     * Counts failed attempts per username and suspends authentication once a username\n     * accumulates too many of them.\n     *\n     * <p>Details that matter:\n     * <ul>\n     *   <li>Failures only count while they fall inside a rolling {@code failureWindow};\n     *       older ones are forgotten, so a slow trickle of typos never locks anybody\n     *       out.</li>\n     *   <li>Crossing {@code maxFailures} locks the account for {@code lockDuration},\n     *       multiplied by {@code backoffFactor} for each consecutive lock, capped at\n     *       {@code maxLockDuration}. The counter resets when the lock trips, so a\n     *       released account gets a fresh budget.</li>\n     *   <li>A lock is lifted only by its own timer or by an operator calling\n     *       {@link #clear(String)}. A later success can never lift it, which keeps the\n     *       lockout meaningful when the correct password is guessed during the window.</li>\n     *   <li>Transitions run inside {@code ConcurrentHashMap.compute}, so concurrent\n     *       attempts against the same username are serialised and cannot race past the\n     *       threshold.</li>\n     *   <li>Entries for usernames that were never real accounts are kept only for\n     *       {@code retention} and then swept, so the map cannot be grown without bound by\n     *       trying random names.</li>\n     * </ul>\n     */\n    public static final class LoginAttemptTracker {\n\n        private final int maxFailures;\n        private final long lockMillis;\n        private final double backoffFactor;\n        private final long maxLockMillis;\n        private final long failureWindowMillis;\n        private final long retentionMillis;\n        private final long purgeIntervalMillis;\n        private final LongSupplier clock;\n\n        private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, State> states = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n        private final Object purgeLock = new Object();\n        private volatile long lastPurgeMillis;\n\n        /** Typical policy: five failures in ten minutes suspends an account for fifteen minutes. */\n        public LoginAttemptTracker(int maxFailures, Duration lockDuration, Duration failureWindow) {\n            this(maxFailures, lockDuration, 1.0d, lockDuration, failureWindow,\n                    failureWindow.plus(lockDuration.multipliedBy(2)), System::currentTimeMillis);\n        }\n\n        public LoginAttemptTracker(int maxFailures, Duration lockDuration, double backoffFactor,\n                                   Duration maxLockDuration, Duration failureWindow,\n                                   Duration retention, LongSupplier clock) {\n            if (maxFailures < 1) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"maxFailures must be at least 1\");\n            }\n            if (lockDuration == null || lockDuration.isNegative() || lockDuration.isZero()) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"lockDuration must be positive\");\n            }\n            if (backoffFactor < 1.0d || Double.isNaN(backoffFactor)) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"backoffFactor must be at least 1.0\");\n            }\n            if (maxLockDuration == null || maxLockDuration.compareTo(lockDuration) < 0) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"maxLockDuration must cover lockDuration\");\n            }\n            if (failureWindow == null || failureWindow.isNegative() || failureWindow.isZero()) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"failureWindow must be positive\");\n            }\n            if (retention == null || retention.isNegative() || retention.isZero()) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"retention must be positive\");\n            }\n            if (clock == null) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"clock must not be null\");\n            }\n            this.maxFailures = maxFailures;\n            this.lockMillis = lockDuration.toMillis();\n            this.backoffFactor = backoffFactor;\n            this.maxLockMillis = maxLockDuration.toMillis();\n            this.failureWindowMillis = failureWindow.toMillis();\n            this.retentionMillis = retention.toMillis();\n            this.purgeIntervalMillis = Math.max(1L, Math.min(this.retentionMillis, 60_000L) / 4L);\n            this.clock = clock;\n            this.lastPurgeMillis = clock.getAsLong();\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Registers a failed attempt.\n         *\n         * @return {@code true} if this failure is the one that locked the account\n         */\n        public boolean recordFailure(String username) {\n            requireKey(username);\n            purgeIfNeeded();\n            boolean[] tripped = new boolean[1];\n            states.compute(username, (key, state) -> {\n                long now = clock.getAsLong();\n                if (state == null) {\n                    state = new State();\n                }\n                state.lastTouchMillis = now;\n                if (state.lockedUntilMillis > now) {\n                    return state; // already suspended: no double counting, no extension\n                }\n                if (state.failures == 0 || now - state.windowStartMillis > failureWindowMillis) {\n                    state.failures = 0;\n                    state.windowStartMillis = now;\n                }\n                state.failures++;\n                if (state.failures >= maxFailures) {\n                    state.consecutiveLocks++;\n                    state.lockedUntilMillis = now + lockDurationMillis(state.consecutiveLocks);\n                    state.failures = 0;\n                    tripped[0] = true;\n                }\n                return state;\n            });\n            return tripped[0];\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Notes a successful login. Failures and the escalating-lock counter are forgotten,\n         * but an active lock survives: only its timer or {@link #clear(String)} ends it.\n         */\n        public void recordSuccess(String username) {\n            requireKey(username);\n            states.compute(username, (key, state) -> {\n                if (state == null) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                if (state.lockedUntilMillis > clock.getAsLong()) {\n                    return state;\n                }\n                return null;\n            });\n        }\n\n        /** Millis of lock left for {@code username}; 0 when authentication is allowed. */\n        public long remainingLockMillis(String username) {\n            requireKey(username);\n            long[] remaining = new long[1];\n            states.compute(username, (key, state) -> {\n                if (state == null) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                long now = clock.getAsLong();\n                if (state.lockedUntilMillis > now) {\n                    remaining[0] = state.lockedUntilMillis - now;\n                    return state;\n                }\n                state.lockedUntilMillis = 0L;\n                if (state.failures == 0 && now - state.lastTouchMillis > retentionMillis) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                if (now - state.windowStartMillis > failureWindowMillis) {\n                    state.failures = 0;\n                }\n                return state;\n            });\n            return remaining[0];\n        }\n\n        /** Failures counted inside the current window; for operators and tests. */\n        public int failureCount(String username) {\n            requireKey(username);\n            int[] count = new int[1];\n            states.compute(username, (key, state) -> {\n                if (state == null) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                long now = clock.getAsLong();\n                if (state.lockedUntilMillis <= now\n                        && now - state.windowStartMillis > failureWindowMillis) {\n                    state.failures = 0;\n                }\n                count[0] = state.failures;\n                return state;\n            });\n            return count[0];\n        }\n\n        /** Operator override: lift a lock and forget the failures for one username. */\n        public void clear(String username) {\n            requireKey(username);\n            states.remove(username);\n        }\n\n        /** Usernames currently held in memory; bounded by {@code retention}. */\n        public int trackedKeys() {\n            return states.size();\n        }\n\n        /** Drops state for usernames idle beyond {@code retention}; swept automatically too. */\n        public int purgeExpired() {\n            long now = clock.getAsLong();\n            int[] removed = new int[1];\n            states.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n                State state = entry.getValue();\n                boolean idle = state.lockedUntilMillis <= now\n                        && now - state.lastTouchMillis > retentionMillis;\n                if (idle) {\n                    removed[0]++;\n                }\n                return idle;\n            });\n            synchronized (purgeLock) {\n                lastPurgeMillis = now;\n            }\n            return removed[0];\n        }\n\n        private long lockDurationMillis(int consecutiveLocks) {\n            long duration = lockMillis;\n            for (int i = 1; i < consecutiveLocks && duration < maxLockMillis; i++) {\n                duration = Math.min(maxLockMillis, (long) (duration * backoffFactor));\n            }\n            return Math.max(lockMillis, Math.min(duration, maxLockMillis));\n        }\n\n        private void purgeIfNeeded() {\n            if (clock.getAsLong() - lastPurgeMillis >= purgeIntervalMillis) {\n                purgeExpired();\n            }\n        }\n\n        private static void requireKey(String username) {\n            if (username == null || username.isEmpty()) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"username must not be null or empty\");\n            }\n        }\n\n        /** Mutable only while held by the enclosing {@code compute}, which serialises per key. */\n        private static final class State {\n            int failures;\n            long windowStartMillis;\n            int consecutiveLocks;\n            long lockedUntilMillis;\n            long lastTouchMillis;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------- store\n\n    /** In-memory {@link AccountStore} used by the demo and the tests. */\n    public static final class InMemoryAccountStore implements AccountStore {\n\n        private final Map<String, Account> accounts = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n        private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n\n        public InMemoryAccountStore(PasswordHasher hasher) {\n            if (hasher == null) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"hasher must not be null\");\n            }\n            this.hasher = hasher;\n        }\n\n        public Account register(String username, char[] password) {\n            return register(username, password, true);\n        }\n\n        public Account register(String username, char[] password, boolean enabled) {\n            if (!isWellFormedUsername(username)) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"username cannot name an account: \" + username);\n            }\n            if (!PasswordHasher.isHashable(password)) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password cannot be hashed safely\");\n            }\n            Account account = new Account(username, hasher.hash(password), enabled);\n            accounts.put(username, account);\n            return account;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Replaces a password. The caller must also lift any lockout with\n         * {@link LoginAttemptTracker#clear(String)}, otherwise a locked account stays\n         * locked after a password reset.\n         */\n        public Account changePassword(String username, char[] password) {\n            Account existing = accounts.get(username);\n            if (existing == null) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"no such account: \" + username);\n            }\n            return register(username, password, existing.enabled());\n        }\n\n        @Override public Optional<Account> find(String username) {\n            return username == null ? Optional.empty() : Optional.ofNullable(accounts.get(username));\n        }\n\n        public int size() { return accounts.size(); }\n    }\n\n    // --------------------------------------------------------------------- demo\n\n    /**\n     * Scripted walkthrough, or a credential prompt with {@code --interactive}.\n     */\n    public static void main(String[] args) {\n        if (args.length > 0 && \"--interactive\".equals(args[0])) {\n            interactive();\n            return;\n        }\n        PasswordHasher hasher = new PasswordHasher();\n        InMemoryAccountStore store = new InMemoryAccountStore(hasher);\n        store.register(\"alice\", \"Corr3ct-Horse-Battery\".toCharArray());\n        store.register(\"bob\", \"hunter2-hunter2!\".toCharArray());\n        store.register(\"svc-batch\", \"d1sabled-s3cret-pw\".toCharArray(), false);\n\n        LoginAttemptTracker tracker = new LoginAttemptTracker(\n                3, Duration.ofSeconds(2), Duration.ofMinutes(10));\n        LoginService service = new LoginService(store, hasher, tracker, AuditSink.println());\n\n        System.out.println(\"Stored record for alice: \" + store.find(\"alice\").orElseThrow().hashedPassword());\n        System.out.println();\n\n        System.out.println(\"[1] correct password\");\n        attempt(service, \"alice\", \"Corr3ct-Horse-Battery\");\n\n        System.out.println(\"[2] wrong password\");\n        attempt(service, \"alice\", \"wrong-password\");\n\n        System.out.println(\"[3] username that does not exist - same wording, same cost\");\n        attempt(service, \"mallory\", \"whatever-123456\");\n\n        System.out.println(\"[4] three failures in a row trip the lock\");\n        attempt(service, \"bob\", \"nope-1\");\n        attempt(service, \"bob\", \"nope-2\");\n        attempt(service, \"bob\", \"nope-3\");\n\n        System.out.println(\"[5] bob is locked, so even the right password is refused\");\n        attempt(service, \"bob\", \"hunter2-hunter2!\");\n\n        System.out.println(\"[6] after the lock expires bob is back in\");\n        sleepMillis(2_100L);\n        attempt(service, \"bob\", \"hunter2-hunter2!\");\n\n        System.out.println(\"[7] a disabled account never authenticates\");\n        attempt(service, \"svc-batch\", \"d1sabled-s3cret-pw\");\n\n        System.out.println(\"[8] an unknown username is throttled by the same policy\");\n        for (int i = 1; i <= 4; i++) {\n            attempt(service, \"admin\", \"guessed-\" + i);\n        }\n\n        System.out.println();\n        System.out.println(\"tracked usernames still held in memory: \" + tracker.trackedKeys());\n    }\n\n    private static void attempt(LoginService service, String username, String password) {\n        char[] candidate = password.toCharArray();\n        LoginOutcome outcome = service.login(username, candidate);\n        System.out.println(\"    -> \" + outcome + \" | \" + outcome.message());\n    }\n\n    private static void interactive() {\n        java.io.Console console = System.console();\n        if (console == null) {\n            System.out.println(\"No console attached; run without --interactive for the scripted demo.\");\n            return;\n        }\n        PasswordHasher hasher = new PasswordHasher();\n nonInjectivePasswordsRefused() {\n        section(\"passwords the JCE would silently rewrite are refused\");\n        Fixture f = fixture(\"alice\", \"Corr3ct-Horse-Battery\");\n        // PBEKeySpec drops trailing NULs, so \"secret\" and \"secret\\0\" would otherwise\n        // derive identically and one person's secret would verify as another's.\n        check(\"trailing NUL is not hashable\", !LoginService.PasswordHasher.isHashable(\"secret\\0\".toCharArray()));\n        check(\"two trailing NULs are not hashable\",\n                !LoginService.PasswordHasher.isHashable(\"secret\\0\\0\".toCharArray()));\n        check(\"trailing NUL cannot log in\", !f.service.login(\"alice\", \"Corr3ct-Horse-Battery\\0\".toCharArray()).granted());\n        // Every unpaired surrogate maps to '?', so all of these would collide with \"q?\".\n        String[] surrogates = {\"q\" + (char) 0xD800, \"q\" + (char) 0xDFFF, \"q\" + (char) 0xDC00 + \"x\",\n                \"\" + (char) 0xDEAD, \"abc\" + (char) 0xD800 + (char) 0xD801};\n        for (String candidate : surrogates) {\n            check(\"unpaired surrogate refused: \" + describe(candidate),\n                    !LoginService.PasswordHasher.isHashable(candidate.toCharArray()));\n            check(\"unpaired surrogate cannot log in: \" + describe(candidate),\n                    !f.service.login(\"alice\", candidate.toCharArray()).granted());\n        }\n        LoginService.PasswordHasher hasher = new LoginService.PasswordHasher(2_000);\n        String stored = hasher.hash(\"collide-me\".toCharArray());\n        check(\"a refused password cannot be stored either\", throwsIAE(() -> hasher.hash(\"bad\\0\".toCharArray())));\n        check(\"the collide-me record still rejects an unpaired surrogate\",\n                !hasher.matches((\"collide-me\" + (char) 0xD800).toCharArray(), salted PBKDF2 digests\");\n        LoginService.PasswordHasher hasher = new LoginService.PasswordHasher(2_000);\n        char[] password = \"same-password-every-time\".toCharArray();\n        String Base64.getDecoder().decode(parts[3]);\n        eq(\"16-byte salt\", 16, salt.length);\n        eq(\"32-byte derived key\", 32, key.length);\n        check(\"salt is not the password\", !first.contains(\"same-password\"));\n        check(\"key is not the password\", !new String(key, java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.US_ASCII)\n                .contains(\"same\"));\n        check(\"two registrations of one password differ (fresh salt)\", !first.equals(second));\n        check(\"both still verify\", hasher.matches(password, first) && hasher.matches(password, second));\n        check(\"iterations are inside the accepted band\",\n                hasher.iterations() >= LoginService.PasswordHasher.MIN_ITERATIONS\n                        && hasher.iterations() <= LoginService.PasswordHasher.MAX_ITERATIONS);\n        check(\"the shipped default is a real cost (>= 100k rounds here)\",\n                LoginService.PasswordHasher.DEFAULT_ITERATIONS >= 100_000);\n        check(\"the timing-equaliser record is a genuine record\",\n                parseable(hasher.hash(\"throwaway-value\".toCharArray())));\n        check(\"constructor rejects an absurd cost\",\n                throwsIAE(() -> new LoginService.PasswordHasher(10)));\n        check(\"constructor rejects an excessive cost\",\n                throwsIAE(() -> new lockTripsAndIsReportedOnTheNextAttempt() {\n        section(\"the lock trips on the threshold failure and reports on the next one\");\n        MutableClock clock = new MutableClock(1_000_000L);\n        LoginService.PasswordHasher hasher = new LoginService.PasswordHasher(2_000);\n        LoginService.InMemoryAccountStore store = new LoginService.InMemoryAccountStore(hasher);\n        store.register(\"bob\", \"right-password\");\n        LoginService.LoginAttemptTracker tracker = tracker(3, 60_000L, 1.0d, 60_000L,\n                Duration.ofHours(1), Duration.ofHours(2), clock);\n        LoginService service = new LoginService(store, hasher, tracker);\n\n        eq(\"failure 1\", LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, attempt(service, \"bob\", \"wrong-1\"));\n        eq(\"failure 2\", LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, attempt(service, \"bob\", \"wrong-2\"));\n        eq(\"failure 3 (the one that trips)\", LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS,\n                attempt(service, \"bob\", \"wrong-3\"));\n        eq(\"counter reset once the lock tripped\", 0, tracker.failureCount(\"bob\"));\n        LoginOutcome locked = service.login(\"bob\", \"wrong-4\".toCharArray());\n        eq(\"attempt 4 sees the lock\", LoginStatus.ACCOUNT_LOCKED, locked.status());\n        eq(\"lock wording\", LoginService.LOCKED_MESSAGE, locked.message());\n        check(\"retry hint is positive\", locked.retryInSeconds() > 0);\n        check(\"retry hint covers the remaining window\", locked.retryInSeconds() >= 60);\n        check(\"lock is active\", tracker.remainingLockMillis(\"bob\") > 0);\n\n        clock.advance(30_000L);\n        eq(\"still locked halfway through\", LoginStatus.ACCOUNT_LOCKED, attempt(service, \"bob\", \"wrong-5\"));\n        clock.advance(31_000L);\n        eq(\"lock has expired\", LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, attempt(service, \"bob\", \"wrong-6\"));\n    }\n\n    private static void successDoesNotLiftALock() {\n        section(\"a later success cannot lift a lock\");\n        MutableClock clock = new MutableClock(500L);\n        Fixture f = lockedFixture(3, 60_000L, clock);\n        lockAccount(f, \"bob\", 3);\n        eq(\"locked\", LoginStatus.ACCOUNT_LOCKED, attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"right-password\"));\n        f.tracker.recordSuccess(\"bob\");\n        eq(\"success left the lock in place\", LoginStatus.ACCOUNT_LOCKED,\n                attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"right-password\"));\n        check(\"lock still has time left\", f.tracker.remainingLockMillis(\"bob\") > 0);\n        clock.advance(61_000L);\n        eq(\"the timer, not the success, ends the lock\", LoginStatus.SUCCESS,\n                attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"right-password\"));\n    }\n\n    private static void lockExpiryRestoresAFreshFailureBudget() {\n        section(\"a released account gets a fresh failure budget\");\n        MutableClock clock = new MutableClock(900L);\n        Fixture f = lockedFixture(2, 100_000L, clock);\n        lockAccount(f, \"bob\", 2);\n        clock.advance(101_000L);\n        eq(\"unlocked again\", LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"wrong-a\"));\n        eq(\"one failure counted, not three\", 1, f.tracker.failureCount(\"bob\"));\n        eq(\"the second failure trips it again\", LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS,\n                attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"wrong-b\"));\n        eq(\"locked on the next attempt\", LoginStatus.ACCOUNT_LOCKED,\n                attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"wrong-c\"));\n    }\n\n    private static void failuresOutsideTheWindowAreForgotten() {\n        section(\"failures outside the rolling window are forgotten\");\n        MutableClock clock = new MutableClock(10L);\n        Fixture f = new Fixture(new LoginService.PasswordHasher(2_000),\n                tracker(3, 60_000L, 1.0d, 60_000L, Duration.ofSeconds(30), Duration.ofMinutes(30), clock));\n        f.store.register(\"bob\", \"right-password\");\n        eq(\"first failure\", LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"wrong-1\"));\n        clock.advance(10_000L);\n        eq(\"second failure\", LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"wrong-2\"));\n        clock.advance(25_000L); // the first failure is now 35s old, outside the 30s window\n        eq(\"third failure\", LoginStatus.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"wrong-3\"));\n        eq(\"only the two in-window failures counted\", 2, f.tracker.failureCount(\"bob\"));\n        eq(\"so no lock engaged\", 0L, f.tracker.remainingLockMillis(\"bob\"));\n        eq(\"and the account is still usable\", LoginStatus.SUCCESS,\n                attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"right-password\"));\n    }\n\n    private static void consecutiveLocksBackOffAndCap() {\n        section(\"consecutive locks escalate and stop at the cap\");\n        MutableClock clock = new MutableClock(1L);\n        Fixture f = new Fixture(new LoginService.PasswordHasher(2_000),\n                tracker(1, 100L, 2.0d, 800L, Duration.ofMinutes(10), Duration.ofMinutes(10), clock));\n        f.store.register(\"bob\", \"right-password\");\n        long[] expected = {100L, 200L, 400L, 800L, 800L};\n        for (int i = 0; i < expected.length; i++) {\n            f.service.login(\"bob\", (\"wrong-\" + i).toCharArray());\n            long remaining = f.tracker.remainingLockMillis(\"bob\");\n            check(\"lock \" + (i + 1) + \" lasts about \" + expected[i] + \"ms (was \" + remaining + \"ms)\",\n                    remaining > expected[i] - 20L && remaining <= expected[i]);\n            clock.advance(expected[i] + 5L);\n            eq(\"lock \" + (i + 1) + \" then expires\", 0L, f.tracker.remainingLockMillis(\"bob\"));\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void operatorCanClearALock() {\n        section(\"an operator can lift a lock\");\n        MutableClock clock = new MutableClock(77L);\n        Fixture f = lockedFixture(2, 600_000L, clock);\n        lockAccount(f, \"bob\", 2);\n        eq(\"locked\", LoginStatus.ACCOUNT_LOCKED, attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"right-password\"));\n        f.tracker.clear(\"bob\");\n        eq(\"cleared\", 0L, f.tracker.remainingLockMillis(\"bob\"));\n        eq(\"login works again immediately\", LoginStatus.SUCCESS,\n                attempt(f.service, \"bob\", \"right-password\"));\n    }\n\n    private static void unknownUsernameStateIsSwept() {\n        section(\"state for usernames that were never real is swept\");\n        MutableClock clock = new MutableClock(5L);\n        LoginService.LoginAttemptTracker tracker = tracker(5, 1_000L, 1.0d, 1_000L,\n                Duration.ofSeconds(30), Duration.ofSeconds(5), clock);\n        for (int i = 0; i < 200; i++) {\n            tracker.recordFailure(\"guessed-name-\" + i);\n        }\n        eq(\"every guess is tracked for now\", 200, tracker.trackedKeys());\n        clock.advance(6_000L);\n        for (int i = 200; i < 400; i++) {\n            tracker.recordFailure(\"guessed-name-\" + i);\n        }\n        check(\"idle entries are reclaimed (\" + tracker.trackedKeys() + \" tracked)\",\n                tracker.trackedKeys() < 400);\n        eq(\"explicit sweep clears the rest\", 0, sweepAll(tracker, clock));\n        eq(\"map is empty\", 0, tracker.trackedKeys());\n        check(\"a locked entry survives the sweep\", lockSurvivesSweep(clock));\n        check(\"null username is rejected and the locked series gets a fresh account per\n     * sample from its own one-strike tracker.\n     */\n    private static void refusalCostsMatchASuccessfulCheck() {\n        section(\"every refusal costs the same work as a successful check\");\n        int iterations = 30_000;\n        LoginService.PasswordHasher hasher = new LoginService.PasswordHasher(iterations);\n        LoginService.InMemoryAccountStore store = new LoginService.InMemoryAccountStore(hasher);\n        LoginService.LoginAttemptTracker openTracker = new LoginService.LoginAttemptTracker(\n                100_000, Duration.ofMinutes(15), Duration.ofHours(12));\n        LoginService open = new LoginService(store, hasher, openTracker);\n        store.register(\"timing-known\", \"timing-secret\");\n unknown = medianMillis(4, () -> requireRefused(open.login(\"timing-unknown\", wrong)));\n        double unhashableCost = medianMillis(4, () -> requireRefused(open.login(\"timing-known\", unhashable)));\n        double malformed = medianMillis(4, () -> requireRefused(open.login(\"not a name\", wrong)));\n\n        MutableClock clock = new MutableClock(4_000L);\n        LoginService.LoginAttemptTracker strict = tracker(1, 600_000L, 1.0d, 600_000L,\n                Duration.ofHours(1), Duration.ofHours(1), clock);\n        LoginService lockedService = new verified on OpenJDK 17.0.20.1).\n\n```sh\njavac -encoding US-ASCII -d out LoginService.java LoginServiceTest.java\n\njava -cp out LoginService              # scripted walkthrough of every path\njava -cp out LoginService --interactive # prompts for a username and password\njava -ea -cp out LoginServiceTest       # the test suite; exit code 0 means pass\n```\n\n`-encoding US-ASCII` matches the sources, which are ASCII-only so that the build does not\ndepend on the machine's locale (`LANG`/`LC_ALL` are often unset, in which case `javac`\ndefaults to a non-UTF-8 charset and rejects any non-ASCII byte).\n\nThe suite prints one line per section, a measured timing line, and a final\n`PASS: 288 checks in Ns`; on failure it lists each broken check and exits 1.\n\n## What the code does\n\n`LoginService.login(String username, char[] password)` is the single entry point. It returns a\n`LoginOutcome` whose `status()` is one of:\n\n| Status | Meaning | Access |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `SUCCESS` | Username names an enabled account and the password's derived key matches. | granted (`granted()` is `true`) |\n| `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` | Wrong password, unknown username, disabled account, malformed username, or an unhashable password. | refused |\n| `ACCOUNT_LOCKED` | Too many recent failures for that username; authentication is suspended, correct password included. `retryInSeconds()` says for how long. | refused |\n\nGrant access on `SUCCESS` and on nothing else. The path is deliberately: validate the\nusername shape, check the lock, validate the password shape, look the account up, derive and\ncompare.\n\n### Password storage\n\n`PasswordHasher` stores passwords as `pbkdf2$<iterations>$<base64 salt>$<base64 key>`:\nPBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, a fresh 16-byte `SecureRandom` salt per password, a 32-byte derived key,\nand 210,000 iterations for new records (about 0.3 s per derivation on a 2026-class x86 core —\nmeasured here at 306 ms). The cost factor is embedded in the record and honoured on\nverification, so raising it for new passwords does not lock anybody out of an old account.\n\n`MessageDigest.isEqual` compares the derived keys; it is constant time, so the comparison\nleaks nothing about the stored key.\n\nTwo shape checks run before hashing. Usernames must match `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,63}`\nand are matched exactly — no trimming, no case folding, because normalising would merge\ndistinct inputs into one account key. Passwords must be 1-1024 chars without a trailing NUL\nor an unpaired surrogate. That second rule is not cosmetic: `PBEKeySpec` converts the `char[]`\nwith its own UTF-8 encoder, which drops *trailing* NULs and maps every unpaired surrogate to\n`?`, so `\"secret\"` and `\"secret\\0\"` would otherwise derive the same key and one person's\nsecret would verify as somebody else's. Such passwords are refused rather than trimmed —\ntrimming would silently change the secret. Interior NULs and correctly paired surrogates\n(emoji, CJK) are fine.\n\nA record that will not parse — corrupt row, truncated base64, cost factor outside\n1,000-5,000,000, wrong key length — yields `false` after paying the normal derivation cost.\nIt never throws, so a damaged row cannot be probed by error message or by stopwatch, and\ncannot become an open door.\n\n### Repeated failed attempts\n\n`LoginAttemptTracker` keeps per-username state and is the part the task singles out:\n\n- **Rolling window.** Failures only count while they fall inside `failureWindow` (10 minutes\n  by default), so a slow trickle of typos never locks anybody out. Three failures 10 and 25\n  seconds apart against a 30-second window count as one, not three.\n- **Threshold and suspension.** The `maxFailures`-th in-window failure (5 by default)\n  suspends authentication for `lockDuration` (15 minutes). While suspended every attempt is\n  refused, including one with the correct password.\n- **The trip reports one attempt late.** The failure that crosses the threshold still answers\n  `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`, with the same generic wording as every other wrong password; the\n  lock becomes visible on the next attempt. The counter resets when the lock trips, so a\n  released account starts from zero.\n- **Escalation.** Each *consecutive* lock is `backoffFactor` longer than the previous one,\n  capped at `maxLockDuration` (both configurable; the shipped convenience constructor keeps\n  it flat).\n- **Only the timer releases a lock.** A later success clears the *failure* count but can\n  never lift an active suspension — otherwise guessing the password during the window would\n  undo the lockout. `clear(username)` is the operator override, and a password reset must be\n  paired with it or the account stays locked.\n- **Failures during a suspension are inert.** They are not counted again and cannot extend\n  the deadline.\n- **Unknown usernames are throttled too.** The same policy accumulates failures for names\n  that do not exist, which is what stops password spraying and enumeration. Their state is\n  retained only briefly and swept, so the map cannot be grown without bound by trying random\n  names.\n- **Concurrency.** Transitions run inside `ConcurrentHashMap.compute`, so they are serialised\n  per username. The suite pins the exact invariant: with 32 concurrent failures, exactly one\n  reports that it tripped the lock.\n\n### What an attacker cannot learn\n\nEvery non-lock refusal returns the same `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` status and the same\n`\"Invalid username or password.\"` wording, whether the cause was a wrong password, a name\nthat does not exist, a disabled account, a malformed username, or an unhashable password.\n\nThose branches would also differ by *cost*: a real check pays a full PBKDF2 derivation, an\nearly return pays nothing. `PasswordHasher.burnWork()` closes that gap by verifying a\nrandomly generated throwaway record on every branch that would otherwise return cheaply, so\nall five refusal shapes cost the same as a genuine check. The equaliser has to be a real\nrecord produced at runtime: a hand-written constant that fails to parse would skip the\nderivation and silently reintroduce the oracle while the comment still claimed otherwise.\n\nThe test suite therefore times each refusal shape against a successful login instead of\ntrusting any of this, and requires every ratio to land in 0.75-1.35. Measured on this\nmachine:\n\n```\nsuccess=43.5ms known-wrong=43.9ms unknown=43.8ms unhashable=44.2ms malformed=44.1ms locked=44.8ms\n```\n\nThe one deliberate exception is `ACCOUNT_LOCKED`, whose message and `retryInSeconds()` do\nconfirm the username exists. That is unavoidable if users are to be told when they may\nreturn; a deployment that would rather not leak it can return `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` for the\nlocked case at the cost of support burden.\n\n`char[]` is used for passwords rather than `String`, and `login` neither mutates nor retains\nthe array it is handed, so callers can wipe their own copy. The `AuditSink` hook sees\nusernames and statuses only — never a credential — and `Account` holds only the encoded\nrecord.\n\n## Test coverage\n\n`LoginServiceTest` runs without a framework: `main` calls 24 sections and prints a summary.\nIt covers the happy path; a nine-entry wrong-password matrix; unknown-user indistinguishability\nby status, wording and retry hint; exact (non-folded, non-trimmed) matching; roughly twenty\nmalformed usernames including SQL-ish and markup strings, asserted never to reach the store or\ncreate tracker state; refused vs. accepted password shapes (trailing NUL, unpaired surrogates,\ninterior NUL, emoji, CJK, 1024 chars); record shape, salt uniqueness and cost-factor\nmigration; eleven corrupt or tampered records, each of which must fail closed without\nthrowing; the full lockout lifecycle against a deterministic clock (trip timing, fresh budget\nafter release, window expiry, backoff cap, operator clear, inert failures during a suspension);\nunbounded-growth defence; disabled accounts; two concurrent races; audit contents; and the six\ntimed cost series above.\n\nTwo conventions keep those results reproducible. Lockout state is shared mutable state, so the\nwrong-password matrix runs against a tracker that cannot lock (5 failures in a 9-entry matrix\notherwise flips the tail to `ACCOUNT_LOCKED` and makes correct code look broken), and the lock\nitself is exercised in its own fixture. Race assertions are order-insensitive: how 24\nconcurrent attempts split between `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` and `ACCOUNT_LOCKED` depends on the\nschedule, so only what holds for every schedule is asserted there, and the exact serialisation\nclaim is measured at the tracker instead.\n\nThe suite was checked against itself with a mutation battery: fifteen weakened variants of\n`LoginService.java` (each equalising charge removed one branch at a time, lock one failure\nlate, success lifts a lock, stray write extends a suspension, window disabled, sweep disabled,\nnon-constant-time compare, static salt, corrupt record trusted, JCE-lossy passwords accepted,\ndisabled account accepted, no username validation). Each must make the suite fail. See the\nresults table below.\n\n## Integrating it\n\n`AccountStore` is the seam: implement `find(username)` against your database or directory and\nkeep the comparison on the constant-time path. Surface a genuine \"no such account\" as\n`Optional.empty()` — never as a lookup failure, because the login path treats an empty result\nas a wrong password and pays the equalising derivation for it. Parameterise the tracker to\nyour policy:\n\n```java\nPasswordHasher hasher = new PasswordHasher();                 // 210k iterations\nInMemoryAccountStore store = new InMemoryAccountStore(hasher);\nstore.register(\"alice\", consolePassword.toCharArray());\n\nLoginAttemptTracker tracker = new LoginAttemptTracker(\n        5, Duration.ofMinutes(15), Duration.ofMinutes(10));   // 5 strikes / 15 min / 10 min window\nLoginService service = new LoginService(store, hasher, tracker, AuditSink.println());\n\nLoginService.LoginOutcome outcome = service.login(username, password);\nif (outcome.granted()) {\n    // ... only SUCCESS gets here\n}\n```\n\nBefore shipping, note what is *not* in here: this is authentication logic, not a session\nlayer, and there is no rate limiting per IP, no CAPTCHA, no password-strength policy, no\nbreached-password check, no TOTP, and no reset flow. The in-memory store is a demo — in\nproduction the tracker must be shared across instances (Redis or similar) or an attacker\nsimply rotates between app nodes, and a real deployment would migrate `PasswordHasher` to\na memory-hard KDF such as Argon2id via a vetted library, keeping the record-migration scheme\nthat already lets old records verify.
